市場焦點
美國
• 10月S&P Global綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖較前月回落，但仍處高檔區間；服務業動
能略為放緩，製造業PMI則升至56.0，創近4年半新高。新訂單增速加快 ，顯示企業需求、投
資及庫存回補動能仍具韌性 ，然而企業售價與投入成本同步上升 ，反映需求改善 、供應鏈延
遲及成本轉嫁效應持續存在 ，通膨降溫進程仍具不確定性 。整體而言 ，市場關注焦點轉向通
膨與利率走勢，高利率環境恐維持較長時間，進而推高股債資產評價波動。
中國
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季 GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期 ，在出口與科技製造業帶動下 ，
短期成長可望維持穩定 ，但若內需未能有效改善 ，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散 ，建議聚焦在
半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈 ，及高端製造相關產業 ，至於消費 、房地產及傳統內需類股 ，

仍需觀察後續刺激政策與消費信心回升情況。
法國
政局動盪
• 法國金融市場因各地抗議活動持續擴大 ，面臨股債雙跌的局面。市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支
出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮 。法國10年期公債殖利率一度突破 5%，法德利差亦擴
大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標仍處於歷史相對低檔 ，遠低於歐
元區主權債務危機期間，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務危機的風險仍相對有限。

美國PMI維持強勁擴張，經濟成長續航但通膨壓力再升溫
• 企業需求強勁支撐美國景氣維持高檔擴張 ：美國10月綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖略低於前月，但仍處於近五年來
的高位區間，顯示企業活動持續高速擴張；其中服務業 PMI為57.9、製造業PMI升至56.0並創53個月新高，新訂單增速
更創逾4年半最佳表現，反映內需穩健、出口需求改善與企業補庫存效應同步發酵，美國第四季經濟動能仍具韌性。
• 成本上升與供應瓶頸推升通膨風險： 企業售價漲幅創2022年8月以來最高，能源、運輸、金屬原料及薪資成本同步上揚 ，
加上供應鏈交期延長與訂單積壓擴大，使企業得以持續轉嫁成本，暗示未來數月消費者通膨可能再度偏離聯準會目標區
間，市場對利率維持較高水準的預期恐因此升溫。
• 後市觀察：景氣韌性有利獲利成長，但通膨可能推升殖利率、壓抑高評價資產。投資配置建議優先布局美國大型科技股 、

美國金融股及工業資本支出受惠族群，可透過NASDAQ 100 ETF、S&P 500 ETF及美國銀行股ETF參與企業獲利成長；
固定收益則以短天期投資級債為主，以降低利率波動風險。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.24

海外資金流入美國市場，支撐美國資產吸引力
• 海外資金持續流入美國：美國財政部8月TIC資料顯示，
整體資金淨流入 1,998億美元，其中民間部門淨流入
1,785億美元，為主要資金來源；外資淨買進美國長
期證券3,007億美元，遠高於美國投資人加碼海外資
產216億美元，反映全球資金在經濟成長與流動性考
量下，對美國金融市場仍維持高度的配置需求。
• 全球資金偏好美國資產： 外資 8月淨買入美國股票
1,319億美元，高於美國公債與公司債 ，顯示海外投
資人仍看好美國企業獲利與經濟基本面 ，雖然短期國
庫券持有量下降，部分跨境美元資金亦出現波動，但
以10月近況來看長天期公債標售仍吸引境外央行 、
保險資金及大型資產管理機構參與，反映高殖利率水
準對長期資金仍具吸引力。在經濟成長維持韌性與企

業獲利支撐下，美國資產仍為全球資金配置的重要核
心市場。
• 市場看法：海外資金流入有助反映美國資產吸引力，
惟後續仍須觀察通膨 、Fed政策及企業獲利表現 。投
資布局建議可以美國投資等級債、美國中短天期公債
及大型AI基礎建設受惠族群為核心配置 ，兼顧收益、
防禦及長期成長潛力。

歷史數據顯示全球股市突破歷史新高後，後續上漲機率高
• 很多人直覺以為見高就是頂，但過去20年的統計資料顯示，全球股市在創下新高後，多數時間選擇繼續向上走。
• 勝率顯著偏高： 全球股市 (MSCI全球指數 )創新高後持有一年 ，獲得正報酬的機率高達 62%，三個月後的正報酬機率更達
71%。美股與台股表現最亮眼，標普500指數創高後一年上漲機率為76%，那斯達克為79%；而台灣股市表現更為驚人，創
高後三個月內上漲的機率高達82%。
• 下檔拉回風險有限：在創高後短期(一個月內)修正幅度其實相當溫和 ，全球多數主要市場的回徹幅度中位數僅 1%～2%左右。
• 突破前高優於連續創新高： 若市場在歷經至少一個月以上的整理後突破前高 ，其未來一年的平均報酬率高達 8.4%，遠高於

連續每天創新高的3.9%。這代表市場經適度整理後的突破，具備更健康的上漲動能。
創新高後的平均遠期報酬率
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.22

顧
市場寬度收窄，由少數巨頭獨撐
• 當前的市場特徵由少數巨頭帶頭衝： 科技股權重較高的指數(如那斯達克、台股、韓股)，長期以來都有少數權重股拉動指數
的現象。自2024年以來，全球13個主要市場中有10個市場在創新高時，參與上漲的股票比例明顯下降 。已開發市場創新高
時高於200日均線的股票比例從過去 75.5%降至65.1%，新興市場更降至 61.3%。這意味著指數看似風光 ，但多數個股並未
同步創高，當漲勢過度集中於少數大型科技巨頭時，整體市場的抗風險能力會相對減弱。
• 小結與投資建議：歷史經驗顯示，創新高本身是強勢市場的特徵，後續繼續走揚的機率遠大於立刻崩盤。然而，鑑於目前市
場寬度收窄、權重高度集中於科技股的現狀 ，盲目追高個股的風險已顯著增加 。投資建議可以利用ETF掌握大盤趨勢，由於

指數上漲主要由龍頭股驅動 ，透過市值型ETF(如追蹤標普500ETF)能直接享受龍頭巨頭帶來的指數創高紅利 ，避免選錯個股
而賺了指數、賠了價差。此外，提防短期股市回檔，可運用定期定額分批布局或等待大盤適度拉回整理再行加碼，降低追高
風險。
創新高時不同水準的市場寬度佔比
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.22
已開發市場和新興市場創新高時的寬度

法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓
• 法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓：法國各地抗議活動持續擴大，最初由學生發起，隨後因生活成本高漲而引發其
他群體響應。在財政空間有限、俄烏戰爭與美伊衝突推升能源價格的背景下，政府難以有效緩解民生成本壓力，法國金融市
場因此面臨股債雙跌的局面。
• 公債殖利率攀升，但系統性風險仍相對可控：市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮。法國10
年期公債殖利率一度突破5%，法德利差亦擴大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標（CISS）仍處於
歷史相對低檔，遠低於歐元區主權債務危機期間，顯示金融市場尚未出現廣泛的系統性壓力，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務
危機的風險仍相對有限。
G7社會動盪指數，法國高居榜首

資料來源： Beco models country，2026.10.19 資料來源： Bloomberg，2026.10.19

資料來源： Bloomberg，2026.10.15 資料來源： tradingeconomics，2026.10.21
日本貿易收支
• 日本貿易逆差擴大，進口增幅高於出口：日本2026年9月貿易逆差擴大至5,366億日圓，較去年同期的2,777億日圓增加93%，
高於市場預期的1,810億日圓，連續第五個月呈現貿易赤字。出口年增16.7%至10.97兆日圓，受惠於半導體相關電子零件、
製造設備及汽車出口成長；惟進口年增18.9%至11.51兆日圓，增幅高於出口，主要受到原油、半導體電子零件及液化天然
氣進口增加帶動。其中，原油進口年增51.1%，部分反映日本在荷姆茲海峽局勢緊張下，積極分散能源採購來源。
• 日本央行本週料維持利率不變：日本央行9月將政策利率調升25個基點至1.25%，創31年來新高。考量前幾次升息對經濟的

傳導效果仍待觀察，加上貿易逆差擴大反映進口成本壓力，多數分析師預期日本央行本週將暫停升息，以評估貨幣緊縮對經
濟與通膨的影響。

中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期：顯示中國經濟在第二季落底後呈現溫和回升 ，從季度表現來看，
一、二、三季增速分別為5.0%、4.3%及4.5%，反映政策支持與外需復甦開始發揮效果 ，全年達成官方4.5%成長目標的機率
明顯提升，第三季經濟改善的主要動能來自出口 ，受惠於全球企業持續擴建 AI基礎設施，及科技產品需求強勁 ，中國高科技
產品出口表現亮眼 ，尤其是積體電路 、伺服器、電腦零組件，及儲存設備等產品增幅顯著 ，高技術製造業維持雙位數成長 。
同時，中國對美國及歐盟出口維持強勁 ，推升貿易順差 走升，為支撐經濟的重要力量 。然而，內需疲弱仍是當前經濟最大隱
憂，房地產調整尚未結束，居民消費與企業投資意願保守，使經濟復甦結構呈現「外熱內冷」的特徵。

• 展望後市：在出口與科技製造業帶動下 ，短期成長可望維持穩定 ，但若內需未能有效改善 ，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散 ，建
議聚焦在半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈 ，及高端製造相關產業 ，至於消費、房地產及傳統內需類股 ，仍需觀察後續刺激政
策與消費信心回升情況。
資料來源：開源證券，2026.10.22

彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高
• 彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高：彭博產業研究認為，中國AI產業未來將由大型科技平台主導 ，其中騰
訊、字節跳動與阿里巴巴憑藉龐大用戶基礎 、數據資源、資金實力及完整生態系統 ，在AI競賽中具備明顯優勢 ，這些企業可將
AI廣泛應用於電商、社群、遊戲、金融與雲端業務，提升營運效率並強化競爭門檻，例如：阿里巴巴利用 AI 驅動電商平台上的
個人化產品推薦，而騰訊則運用 AI 優化短影音廣告並強化微信功能。騰訊自 2026 年 7 月起開始將混元 AI 模型整合至其電子
遊戲中，使遊戲角色及助理能更準確地回應玩家 ，然而，AI模型訓練與基礎建設投入龐大 ，獲利能力短期仍受壓抑 ，且須面對
監管、資料隱私及晶片供應等挑戰 。此外，智譜、月之暗面、MiniMax及商湯等AI新創雖具技術創新能力，但缺乏規模效益、

穩定現金流及長期資本支持，未來獲利挑戰較大。
• 投資建議：看好中國AI長期發展者，可優先關注具資金 、算力與應用場景優勢的大型平台企業 ，受惠程度可望高於多數 AI新創
公司。

年底集團作帳及選舉行情，正向看待年底台股表現。

Document 067: 2027年春闘、初動のモメンタムは現時点で良好 ～労働側、経営側とも昨年からのトーンダウンはみられず～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2027年春闘、初動のモメンタムは現時点で良好 ～労働側、経営側とも昨年からのトーンダウンはみられず～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年10 月 22 日

1/5 
2027 年春闘、初動のモメンタムは現時点で良好 
～労働側、経営側とも昨年からのトーンダウンはみられず～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

（要旨） 
○ 連合は2027 年春闘基本構想で、 定昇込みの賃上げ要求を 「６％中心、５％以上」 、ベア等の
賃上げ分を「４％中心、３％以上」とする目安を示した。前年よりも要求姿勢をやや強めて
おり、５％台の賃上げの定着に加え、さらなる引き上げを目指す姿勢がうかがえる。 
○ 経団連も高い賃上げの継続に前向きな姿勢を維持している。経労委報告の骨子案では賃上げ
モメンタムの「浸透・定着」が掲げられ、高い賃上げの継続に加え、中小企業への波及を重
視する姿勢が示されている。 
○ 日本経済研究センターのＥＳＰフォーキャスト調査によると、民間エコノミストは 2027 年
春闘の賃上げ率を4.96％と予想（厚生労働省ベース）。前年から小幅に鈍化するものの、高
い伸びが続く見通しであり、４年連続で５％台の賃上げを実現できるかが焦点となる。 
○ 春先にはイラン情勢悪化による賃上げ鈍化が懸念されていたが、現時点で労使双方の姿勢に
トーンダウンはみられない。企業業績の底堅さや構造的な人手不足が高い賃上げを支えてお
り、2027 年春闘の初動のモメンタムは良好と評価できる。

連合は、「６％中心、５％以上」と昨年からトーンをやや強める 
2027 年春闘の集中回答日は2027 年の３月中旬だが、実際の交渉に向けての動きは既に始まりつ
つある。 
本日、連合は「2027 春季生活闘争基本構想」を公表した。春闘に向けて最初に注目される重要
イベントであり、翌年の春闘に向けた労働組合側の基本理念や方針、重点課題を示すものであ
る。この基本構想をみることで、労働側が翌年の春闘にどの程度強気な姿勢で臨もうとしている
かを早い段階で確認することができ、春闘全体のトーンをつかむうえで重要な材料となる。 
今回の基本構想の最大の注目点は、定期昇給相当分を含めて「６％中心、５％以上」 、ベースア
ップ等の賃上げ分を「４％中心、３％以上」とし、より高い賃上げを目指す姿勢を示したことで
ある。「５％以上」という要求水準自体は４年連続（2023 年は「５％程度」）となるが、今回は
「６％中心」との表現が新たに加わった。ベア等の賃上げ分についても、従来の「３％以上」に

### Page 2

2/5 
「４％中心」が加えられている。前年の2026 年春闘では、全体で「５％以上」 、中小で「６％以
上」 、有期・短時間・契約等労働者では「７％」を目安とする、いわゆる「５・６・７」の要求が
示されていた。今回、全体についても６％を明確に意識する形となったことは、実質的には５％
を最低ラインとしつつ、より高い賃上げを促す姿勢を強めたものと受け取れる。単に高い賃上げ
を続けるだけでなく、その水準をさらに引き上げようとする意欲がうかがえる。

こうした姿勢の背景にあるのが、ここ数年の高い賃上げ実績である。2026 年春闘では連合ベー
スの賃上げ率の実績が5.01％となり、３年連続で５％台の高い賃上げが実現した。５％という水
準がかつてほど実現困難な目標ではなくなりつつあるなか、連合としても前年と同じ要求目安を
繰り返すだけでは、さらなる賃上げを促すメッセージとして十分ではないと判断した可能性があ
る。５％台の賃上げを４年目も維持するだけでなく、６％近辺を意識して賃上げ率のさらなる引
き上げを目指す段階に入ったというメッセージと位置付けられる。 
賃上げを取り巻く環境も、こうした強気の姿勢を支えている。構造的な人手不足が続くなか、
企業にとって人材の確保・定着は引き続き重要な経営課題であり、賃金引き上げの必要性は高
い。また、物価上昇率は2026 年度後半にかけて再び高まるとみられ、家計の生活コスト負担は重
い。名目賃金が増加しても、物価上昇に追いつかなければ生活水準の改善にはつながらない。実
質賃金を安定的にプラスにするためにも、労働側が高い賃上げを求める理由は十分にある。 
加えて、賃上げの広がりという点でも課題は残る。2026 年春闘では中小組合の賃上げ率が
4.69％と高い伸びを確保したものの、全体の5.01％には届かなかった。大企業と中小企業の間に
はなお差があり、正規雇用と非正規雇用の賃金格差も残っている。連合にとっては、高い賃上げ
春闘年 連合の要求目安（定昇相当分込み） 連合ベース 厚労省ベース
2020 ４％程度 1.90％ 2.00％
2021 ４％程度 1.78％ 1.86％
2022 ４％程度 2.07％ 2.20％
2023 ５％程度 3.58％ 3.60％
2024 ５％以上 5.10％ 5.33％
2025 ５％以上 5.25％ 5.52％
2026 ５％以上 5.01％ 5.18％
2027 ６％中心、５％以上 — —
(出所）連合「春季生活闘争基本構想」、連合「春季生活闘争 第7回（最終）回答集計結果」、厚生労働省「民間主要企業春季賃上げ要求・妥結状況」
連合の賃上げ要求目安と春闘賃上げ率
実績

### Page 3

3/5 
を実現するだけでなく、それを幅広い企業や労働者に波及させることも引き続き重要な課題であ
る。今回の基本構想でも、賃金実態を把握できないなどの事情がある中小組合については
「20,000 円以上・６％以上」 、有期・短時間・契約等労働者については「７％」を目安とする方針
が示された。こうした点を踏まえれば、今回の基本構想は、高い賃上げの持続と一段の引き上
げ、そして賃上げの裾野の拡大を目指すものと評価できる。 
また、今回の基本構想では、新たに2030 年の賃金水準を2026 年比で15％程度引き上げる中期
目標も設定された。毎年の賃上げ率だけでなく、中期的に目指す賃金水準を明示することで、高
い賃上げを一過性のものに終わらせず、継続させようとする姿勢がうかがえる。 
なお、近年は個別組合の実際の賃上げ要求率がすでに６％近くに達しており、 「６％中心」とい
う今回の表現には、こうした実態を追認する意味合いもある。今回の基本構想からは、連合の強
い賃上げ意欲が確認できる一方、実際の賃上げ率が前年を上回るかどうかはまた別問題である。
今後は、主要産業別労働組合組織や個別組合の要求水準が前年からどの程度引き上げられるか
（られないか）が注目される。

経営側も賃上げの継続に前向き 
経営側も高い賃上げの継続に前向きな姿勢を維持している。経団連が毎年１月に公表する「経
営労働政策特別委員会報告（経労委報告） 」は、春闘に向けた経営側の基本スタンスを示すもので
あり、賃上げ交渉の方向性を占う上で重要な資料となる。2027 年春闘に向けた経労委報告につい
ては、現時点で原案は明らかになっていないものの、９月上旬には骨子案の内容が報道されてい
る。それによると、賃上げモメンタムの「浸透・定着」を掲げ、高い賃上げの継続と中小企業へ
の波及を促す方針とされる。 
経団連は2023 年を賃上げの「起点」 、2024 年を「加速」 、2025 年を「定着」 、2026 年を「さらな
る定着」の年と位置付けてきた。今回の「浸透・定着」という表現からは、高い賃上げを継続す
るとともに、その動きを中小企業などに広げることを重視する姿勢がうかがえる。また、経団連
の筒井会長は９月７日の記者会見で、 「ベースアップ実施の検討を引き続きスタンダードと位置付
けていきたい」と述べ、賃上げのモメンタムを維持する考えを示している。 
数か月前には、イラン情勢の悪化による原材料費や物流費の上昇が企業収益を圧迫し、2027 年
春闘では経営側が賃上げに慎重な姿勢を強めることも懸念されていた。しかし、現時点で経営側
の賃上げ姿勢にトーンダウンはみられない。 
なお、経労委報告では、原案から正式な報告にかけての修正は例年あまり見られないが、骨子
案から原案にかけては内容が多少修正されることもある。今後、10 月末～11 月中頃までに報道さ
れるとみられる経労委報告の原案が明らかになれば、経営側のスタンスをより具体的に判断でき
るだろう。

エコノミスト予想は4.96％、５％を巡る攻防か 
10 月14 日に日本経済研究センターが公表した「ＥＳＰフォーキャスト調査」では、民間エコノ

### Page 4

4/5 
ミスト 34 人を対象に2027 年春闘の賃上げ率について特別調査を実施している。これによると、
予測値の平均は4.96％となり、2026 年実績の5.18％を 0.22％ポイント下回る見通しとなった。
もっとも、前年から小幅に鈍化するとはいえ、ほぼ５％という歴史的に高い水準であり、2027 年
も高い賃上げが続くとの見方が中心である。なお、ここでの賃上げ率は厚生労働省「民間主要企
業春季賃上げ要求・妥結状況」ベースであり、連合の集計とは対象が異なる。 
予測の内訳をみると、定期昇給分が1.69％、ベースアップ分が3.27％となっており、ベアにつ
いても３％を超える伸びが見込まれている。また、予測値の高位８機関平均は5.23％、低位８機
関平均は4.68％となっている。予測には一定の幅があるものの、高い賃上げが続くという点では
概ね認識は共有されている。

2027 年春闘で賃上げ率が前年からやや鈍化すると予想される背景には、2026 年の物価上昇率が
2025 年より低く、物価高に対応するための賃上げ圧力が幾分弱まることがあると考えられる。ま
た、数年にわたる高い賃上げによって人件費負担が累積していることに加え、イラン情勢悪化を
背景とした原材料費や物流費の上昇も、企業の賃上げ余力を抑制する要因となり得る。一方、企
業業績が足元まで好調を維持していることや、構造的な人手不足と採用競争の激化は引き続き賃
上げを促す要因であり、賃上げ率が大きく鈍化する可能性も低いとみられる。 
ちなみに、昨年の同時期のＥＳＰフォーキャスト調査でも、2026 年春闘の賃上げ率は4.81％
（2025 年10 月調査）と、前年実績からの鈍化が予想されていた。しかし、実際には5.18％と予
想を上回り、３年連続で５％台の賃上げが実現した。今回も前年からの小幅な鈍化が見込まれて
いるが、予測平均は昨年の同時期をやや上回っている。イラン情勢の悪化という新たな懸念材料
があるなかでも、エコノミストの間では高い賃上げが続くとの見方が維持されていることが分か

（注）2027年は予測値、2026年までの実績は厚生労働省「民間主要企業春季賃上げ要求・妥結状況」

### Page 5

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
5/5 
る。 
こうしたなか、予測平均の4.96％について、前年からの「鈍化」を強調するか、5％近傍という
「高水準の持続」を重視するかは、評価が分かれるところだろう。いずれにせよ、2027 年春闘で
は、３年連続で実現した５％台の賃上げを４年目も維持できるか、５％を巡る攻防が一つの焦点
となる。今後、経労委報告原案の判明により経営側の方針が明らかになるほか、主要産業別労働
組合組織の要求方針などが明らかになるにつれて、賃上げ率の見通しもより具体化していくこと
になるだろう。 
なお、この調査とは別に、毎年11 月～12 月にかけて、民間シンクタンク・調査機関から翌年の
春闘賃上げ率の予測が出されることが多い （筆者は 11 月に発表予定） 。 予測をレポートとして作成
することもあれば、 経済見通しレポートのなかで言及されることもあり、春闘賃上げ率の見通しに
ついて、この時期に市場の相場観が徐々に形成されていく。

2027 年春闘の初動は良好 
以上を踏まえると、2027 年春闘の「初動のモメンタム」は、現時点では良好と評価できる。イ
ラン情勢が悪化した数か月前には、原材料費や物流費の上昇による企業収益の圧迫を通じて、
2027 年春闘の賃上げ率が大きく鈍化するとの懸念もあった。しかし、ここまでの動きをみる限
り、労働側、経営側のいずれについても、前年と比べて賃上げ姿勢が慎重化している様子はみら
れない。連合が定昇込み、ベアともに前年より強い要求目安を打ち出し、さらに高い賃上げを志
向する一方、経団連も高い賃上げの継続と中小企業への波及に前向きな姿勢を示している。 
その背景には、原油などの代替調達が進み、資源の調達難が生産活動を大きく制約する事態が
回避されたことや、ＡＩ需要の拡大にも支えられ、企業業績が総じて堅調さを維持していること
があるとみられる。また、構造的な人手不足や採用競争の激化によって、企業が人材確保・定着
のために賃金を引き上げる必要性は依然として高い。物価上昇が続くなか、実質賃金の改善を求
める声が強いことも、高い賃上げに向けての動きを後押ししているとみられる。 
もっとも、原材料費や物流費の上昇による企業収益への悪影響が今後さらに広がる可能性はあ
り、先行きを楽観視することはできない。また、現時点で確認できるのは労使双方の基本的な姿
勢であり、具体的な要求や回答の水準はまだ明らかになっていない。今後は、経団連「経労委報
告」の原案や主要産業別労働組合組織の要求方針、早期に賃上げを表明する企業の動向などを通
じて、今回確認された前向きな姿勢が実際の賃上げにつながるかを見極める必要がある。

Stronger revenue momentum while GM guidance came in softer 
TSMC hosted its 3Q26 analyst meeting on October 15. While we 
view the higher full-year 2026 revenue guidance and the further 
acceleration in capacity buildout as the key positives, the GM 
guidance could be the softer spot. In the meeting, management 
further raised its 2026 revenue growth outlook to grow by around 
44% YoY (from slightly above 40%; USD term), supported by 
continued strong demand especially for AI/HPC. However, both 
3Q26 GM result and 4Q26 GM guidance came in lower than our 
expectations; we believe this is to reflect a steeper N2 ramp and 
overseas fab dilution in the near term. 
AI demand still ahead of supply, with no sign of slowdown 
Key questions among investors largely surrounded demand outlook. 
Overall, we think that management’s tone on AI demand remained 
very bullish. While TSMC did not formally update its long-term AI 
accelerator revenue CAGR, management indicated the CAGR will 
probably be higher, noting demand is extremely robust with no sign 
of slowdown and still far exceeds supply, with multiple drivers 
reinforcing this view, including - 1) agentic AI driving incremental 
CPU demand, which is excluded from TSMC’s AI accelerator 
definition and implies upside beyond the headline CAGR; 2) broader 
adoption of open-source models lowering the barrier to AI usage 
and lifting leading-edge silicon demand; 3) continued demand for 
networking chips alongside AI accelerators; and 4) TSMC’s own 
checks on US data center build progress and power supply, where 
management sees CSPs as very well prepared.  
Capacity build-out accelerating again: N2 capacity more than 
double in 2027; additional N3 fab addition; Texas under evaluation

### Page 3

more than double in 2027. As for N3, management announced an additional 3nm fab
expansion in Tainan on strong customer demand, with volume production in 1H28. This
comes on top of 1) the ongoing N3 fab expansion in Tainan (ramp in 1H27), 2) Arizona
fab 2 (ramp in 3Q27, slightly ahead of its prior schedule), 3) Japan fab 2 (ramp in 2028),
and 4) continued N5-to-N3 tool conversion. Beyond Arizona, TSMC confirmed it is
evaluating Texas as a potential additional US site, and is in discussion with one customer

building Terafab on utilizing that capacity; while the business model and structure
remains under discussion, TSMC highlighted that its principle is to run the fab by itself.
GM: steeper N2 dilution near term, but “no ceiling” long term
3Q26 GM declined 1.3ppt QoQ to 66.4%, primarily on dilution from the initial N2 ramp,
and 4Q26 GM is guided down a further 40bps to 66.0% at the mid-point on continued
N2 dilution and overseas fabs. Management now expects overseas fab dilution to be

closer to 2% for full-year 2026 (vs. 2-3% previously), helped by larger scale and cost
improvement, but reiterated it will widen to 3-4% in the latter part of the five-year
period as more US fabs ramp. Longer term, management stated there is “no ceiling” for
TSMC’s profitability, though any dramatic increase will not happen overnight. On pricing,
management reiterated a strategic approach by segment, location and technology, with
US production priced differently from Taiwan. Overall, we slightly lower our GM to

66.6%/66.8%/67.7% in 2026-2028E (vs. 67.2%/67.5%/67.8% previously).
CapEx: 2026 unchanged, multi-year step-up reiterated
2026 CapEx guidance was unchanged at US$60-64bn. While management did not
provide numeric guidance beyond 2026, but reiterated that 2026-2028 CapEx will be
significantly higher than the prior three years, and that revenue growth should continue
to outpace CapEx growth. Management acknowledged the tools, material and labor
inflation, but said the majority of CapEx is driven by long-term demand. Net net, we

maintain our 2026E/2027E/2028E CapEx at US$64bn/US$85bn/US$98bn.
High confidence in its structural lead
Addressing questions regarding rising competition, management remains confident in its
technology lead  for both front end wafer and advanced packaging technology. Despite
the later adoption timeframe (planned for in 2030) vs peers on High NA EUV, TSMC does
not see the later adoption than peers as a disadvantage. In advanced packaging, TSMC is
in volume production at 5x reticle size, which management believes no peer can match

today, with 9x and 14x in development and CoPoS for future cost reduction. In addition,
management also highlighted that it expects A14 (volume production in 2028) to be a
bigger node than N2, with customer engagement already exceeding N2’s at the same
stage.

### Page 5

3Q26 results and 4Q26 guidance recap

4Q26 guidance: revenue well above expectations, GM below GSe 
For 4Q26, management guided revenue to US$52.3-54.3bn (+14% QoQ at the 
mid-point; based on FX [TWD/USD] of 31.8), GM to 65-67% and OpM to 56.5-58.5%. 
Revenue guidance came in well above GSe of +11.0% QoQ, supported by continued 
strong leading-edge demand including the steep N2 ramp. However, the GM mid-point 
of 66.0% is 1.3ppt below GSe of 67.3% and broadly in line with consensus of 66.1%, as 
continued N2 dilution and overseas fabs are only partly offset by productivity gains and 
cross-node capacity optimization. 
3Q26 results: revenue and EPS beat, GM below GSe 
3Q26 revenue of NT$1,494.2bn (US$46.72bn) was up 17.6% QoQ and 50.9% YoY, above 
the high end of guidance in USD terms and above GSe/consensus by 0.8%/2.1%. EPS of 
NT$29.43 was up 8.0% QoQ and 68.7% YoY, above GSe/consensus by 1.2%/3.7%; 
excluding the NT$2.24 one-off gain from the VIS disposal in 2Q26, EPS grew 18% QoQ. 
GM of 66.4% (-1.3ppt QoQ) landed in the upper half of guidance (65-67%) but 1.1ppt 
below GSe, while OpM of 58.7% beat the high end of guidance (56-58%) on stable OpEx 
at 7.8% of revenue. 
By technology, N2 contributed 8% of wafer revenue (vs. 3% in 2Q26) and N3 33% (vs. 
30%), taking advanced nodes (7nm and below) to 79% (vs. 77%). By platform, HPC rose 
22% QoQ to 68% of revenue (vs. 66%), with smartphone +10% QoQ at 21%. Inventory 
days fell 5 days QoQ to 82 days on higher wafer shipments.

### Page 7

Reiterate Buy; 12m TP maintain at NT$3,300 
We maintain our 12m TP at NT$3,300  based on a target P/E of 22x (unchanged, 
benchmarked to 1.0stdv above 5-year avg. forward P/E) applied to our 2027E EPS. 
Accordingly, we maintain our 12 month TP for the ADR at US$660, based on an 
unchanged FX conversion of USD/TWD of 30.0 and unchanged ADR premium of 20%. 
Our 22x target P/E multiple is also benchmarked to TSMC’s trading average of 22x 
during its previous revenue upcycle where its 3-year revenue CAGR (2019-2021) was 
28%, vs. our 3-year revenue CAGR at 38% during 2025-2028E. 
Our TP implies 35% (ADR 54%) upside to the last close. As a result, we reiterate our Buy 
ratings for TSMC/ADR. We maintain our constructive view on TSMC’s long-term growth 
outlook as we believe the company’s solid technology leadership and execution better 
positions it vs. peers to capture the industry’s long-term structural growth 
opportunities, especially in AI.

### Page 9

Investment Thesis, Price Target Risks and Methodology

Investment Thesis - TSMC (2330.TW/TSM) 
TSMC is a leading global foundry company specializing in cutting-edge nodes. Its leading 
technology stance enables it to enjoy more than 60% revenue share in the global 
foundry market. We like TSMC as we believe its solid technology leadership and 
execution better position it vs. peers to capture the industry’s long-term structural 
growth opportunities, particularly in areas such as AI/5G/HPC/EV. In addition, valuation 
looks attractive in our view, with the shares trading at the mid-range of their 10-year 
trading history (P/E: 10-29x). Furthermore, we view TSMC as the key AI enabler among 
our Taiwan Semis coverage, thanks to its leadership stance in leading edge nodes and 
advanced packaging technology – CoWoS (chip on wafer on substrate). We believe TSMC 
will achieve its 25% revenue CAGR target for the next several years, driven primarily by 
increasing silicon content growth and AI/HPC demand, with LT GM to remain at 56%+. 
We are Buy rated.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - TSMC (2330.TW/TSM) 
Valuation methodology: We have a 12m TP of NT$3,300, which is derived by applying a 
target P/E multiple of 22x to our 2027E EPS. Our 22x target P/E is benchmarked against 
1.0stdv above its 5-year trading average. For the ADR (TSM), we have a 12m TP of 
US$660, based on a USD/TWD rate of 30.0 and an ADR premium of 20%. 
Key downside risks to our views: (1) further deterioration in end-demand recovery 
impacting capacity utilization; (2) slower customer node migrations; (3) slowdown in AI 
investment resulting in lower long-term semiconductor content growth; (4) poor 
yields/execution resulting in worse-than-expected profitability; (5) stronger 
competition resulting in ASP/profitability erosion; and (6) unfavorable FX trend or 
higher-than-expected cost increase weighing on the margin outlook.
15 October 2026   9

their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Idea
TSMC  | Asia Pacific
No slowdown of AI semi
demand; reiterate OW
TSMC is further expanding capacity after considering other
constraints, such as data center build, power and other
components supply.  We think the risk of over-capacity is small,
and stay OW.
TSMC sees robust AI semi demand: Despite CSP power shortages and rising
financing costs, TSMC sees demand far above its capacity. It sees no signs of a
slowdown, which may ease some market concerns. The company did not update its

AI semi CAGR guidance (55-60%), saying it is not meaningful as  the demand is
expanding rapidly.
Comments on  wafer foundry partnership (TeraFab) and competition (Intel): For
overseas fabs, TSMC is evaluating a TeraFab partnership, but TSMC must run the
partner fab. TSMC is also considering other fab campus locations, including Texas.
We asked management about IDM outsourcing from Intel and implications for A14
capacity demand. Management said it would try to be fair to all customers. On

competition, TSMC would expand capacity and “leave no food on the table” for
competitors.
On advanced packaging, we asked whether TSMC would help customers using
Intel EMIB-T advanced packaging: The company is confident about 9x and 14x
reticle size chip support. TSMC’s principle is to help customers ramp products
smoothly, so we take it as no objection if Google/MediaTek needs to adopt CoWoS/
CoPoS in future TPU versions, as we articulated in MediaTek: TSMC CoWoS support

eases MediaTek's 2028 TPU production concerns
We raise 2027/28 capex estimates to factor in 2nm and 1.4nm expansion: The
company maintained its 2026 capex guidance (US$60-64bn) and didn’t provide
next-year capex guidance. That said, it  mentioned more than a doubling of 2nm
capacity build by 2027.  Our initial cross checks with equipment venders suggest
EUV tool bookings double in 2027 (vs. 2026), followed by 25-30% growth in 2028.
We therefore raise our 2027/28 capex assumption to US$83/92bn.

3Q26 results review – margin miss, but revenue upside: Pease see Exhibit 1  and
Exhibit 2  for our earnings review. We lower our 2026 and 2027 margin
assumptions,  offset by higher revenue. We maintain our price target at NT$3,088
and reiterate our OW rating.

TSMC 3Q26 earnings and 4Q26 guidance  review
3Q26 results and 4Q26 guidance review – margin miss, but revenue upside: 3Q26
margin of 66.4% misses our forecast of 67.5%, but was largely in-line with consensus,
given the margin dilution of 2nm node and overseas operation dilution.
The 4Q26 margin midpoint of 66% shows continued pressure. TSMC guides for 4Q26
revenue to grow 14% Q/Q at the midpoint on very strong demand, in line with our 10-15%

Q/Q expectation. Full-year revenue guidance is raised to 44% Y/Y, also similar to our
preview.

TSMC: Earnings estimate revisions
Our earnings forecasts decline 3% for 2026,  3% for  2027 and is largely unchanged  for
2028: We lower our 2026/27 earnings forecast mostly due to the margin miss of 3Q26
results and weaker than our initial expectation of 4Q26 margin guidance, and also the
wider overseas fab margin dilution into 2027. We slightly raise the revenue assumption for
2027/2028 as TSMC suggested it would speed up the 3nm/2nm capacity build, but that

also comes with larger depreciation and margin pressure when new production ramps.

Our PT is unchanged at NT$3,088. Our PT implies a 20x P/E multiple on our new 2027

EPS forecast. TSMC currently trades at 17x that estimate, close to its average NTM P/E of
16.5x since 2018. We find the stock attractive at current levels.
We continue to derive our 12-month price target (base-case scenario) using a residual
income valuation framework. Our key assumptions are unchanged, including a cost of
equity of 9.2% (beta of 1.2, equity risk premium of 6.0%, and risk-free rate of 2.0%), an
intermediate growth rate of 10.5%, and a terminal growth rate of 4%.

Our bull and bear case scenario values remain at NT$3,720 and NT$1,700, respectively.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

EQUITY: FABLESS
Global Unichip Corp   3443.TW 3443 TT
Global Markets Research
19 October 2026
Riding the next megatrend of SoIC; maintain Buy
Several key CSP projects in the pipeline to support growth until 2028-29F
Benefiting from SoIC — the next megatrend in advanced packaging, in our view
GUC shares have rallied ~100% since late July (vs the TAIEX up 11%), driven by a strong 
wave of consensus earnings upward revisions (Fig. 11). This is largely in line with our 
observations highlighted in our 2 Aug report that “consensus expectations were way too low.” 
We believe the Street is starting to acknowledge that there is a strong pipeline of CSP projects 
over the next 2-3 years, including Google CPU (GOOGL US, Not rated; revenue sharply 
higher in 2026 with momentum potentially sustaining into 2027F and 2028F as Axion 3 
doubles silicon content from Axion 2), as well as the other top three US hyperscalers’ ASIC 
and CPU projects along with Tesla’s (TSLA US, Not rated) AI5/AI6.5. GUC not only has 
secured projects from top US hyperscalers, but at the same time acquired additional 
meaningful customers with volume globally, e.g. China auto ASIC and US AI ASIC using a 
SoW approach. However, among all, the one that has caught our attention the most is the 
Google TPU v.10 compute die opportunity (though we believe the turnkey deal is yet to be 
finalized) — not only because the addressable size could be significant, but this business 
opportunity might originate from GUC’s G-link 3D die-on-die IP. (Fig. 1 Fig. 4) We raise our 
2026-28F EPS for GUC by 15%-37% to incorporate business upside from non-Axion projects, 
and raise TP to TWD9,600 (from TWD5,800), based on an unchanged 40x target P/E, while 
rolling forward valuation to the 2027-28F average EPS. We are yet to incorporate overseas 
convertible bonds (ECB) in our model/EPS estimates.
The rising importance of SoIC in advanced packaging should benefit GUC, given its 
leading investments in Glink-3D die-on-die IP among design service peers
SoIC is not new, but it was small before 2026 given limited adoption by AMD MI300 chip (AMD 
US, Not rated) and Apple M-series chip (AAPL US, Not rated). In 2026, nVidia’s (NVDA US, Not 
rated) demand for CPO using TSMC’s (2330 TT, Buy) COUPE (EIC on PIC stacking) has further 
lifted SoIC demand. In our June anchor report, we noted that nVidia’s Feynman could become 
the first GPU-on-GPU stacking SoIC (along with SiC carrier) AI chip. Since then, several US CSP 
ASIC projects have also planned to enable SoIC die stacking in their chip roadmap, according to 
our industry checks. GUC’s Glink-3D IP (see Fig. 1- Fig. 4) appears well positioned to ride this 
trend as it has developed this IP along with TSMC’s roadmap and currently a number of CSPs 
are using its IP to build their test chip – which in the long run could prove to be a good stepping 
stone for GUC to win more services, in our view. Besides, GUC’s SoIC IP would also be 
deployed with TSMC’s scale-up CPO customers (e.g. AyarLabs, Fig. 5).

### Page 3

Company profile
Established in 1998, GUC is one of the leading design service companies in Taiwan with self-developed IPs. TSMC is GUC's single-largest
shareholder with a 35% share.
Valuation Methodology
Our TP of TWD9,600 is based on 40x 2027-28F average EPS (40x P/E is its historical mid level). The benchmark index for the stock is TAIEX.
Risks that may impede the achievement of the target price

Definition of Nomura Group's equity research rating system and sectors
The rating system is a relative system, indicating expected performance against a specific benchmark identified for each individual stock, 
subject to limited management discretion. An analyst’s target price is an assessment of the current intrinsic fair value of the stock based on an 
appropriate valuation methodology determined by the analyst. Valuation methodologies include, but are not limited to, discounted cash flow 
analysis, expected return on equity and multiple analysis. Analysts may also indicate expected absolute upside/downside relative to the stated 
target price, defined as (target price - current price)/current price.

SECTORS
A 'Bullish' stance, indicates that the analyst expects the sector to outperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Neutral' stance, 
indicates that the analyst expects the sector to perform in line with the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Bearish' stance, indicates that 
the analyst expects the sector to underperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. Sectors that are labelled as 'Not rated' or shown as 
'N/A' are not assigned ratings. Benchmarks are as follows: United States: S&P 500; Europe: Dow Jones STOXX 600; Global Emerging 
Markets (ex-Asia): MSCI Emerging Markets ex-Asia. Japan/Asia ex-Japan: Sector ratings are not assigned.

Target Price
A Target Price, if discussed, indicates the analyst’s forecast for the share price with a 12-month time horizon, reflecting in part the analyst's 
estimates for the company's earnings. The achievement of any target price may be impeded by general market and macroeconomic trends, and 
by other risks related to the company or the market, and may not occur if the company's earnings differ from estimates.

研 究  報 告                
力積電(6770.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
記憶體漲價貢獻 4Q26 獲利維持高檔，規劃 3D AI Foundry 新廠  
 投資評價與建議 
3Q26獲利優於預期，且4Q26毛利率有望重回50%以上之高峰，加上明年記憶
體、成熟製程市況展望正向，故我們給予2.3倍PB，持續看好後續營運動能。 
 重點評論分析 
看好4Q26毛利率重回過往50%以上高峰： 基於 ： (1)IPC 、 網通和醫療等應用支
撐力積電DRAM代工需求，Flash業務中，SLC NAND則因記憶體大廠先進製
程產能排擠，獲得車用、網通等小容量應用降規需求挹注；(2) AI伺服器持續
支撐PMIC、分離式元件需求，另考量中介層與Wafer on Wafer於新竹廠區陸
續復線，將有利於3D AI Foundry營收動能回溫。預估有望季增18.3%至252.4
億元，毛利率53.6%，稅後EPS 1.86元。  
正向看待明年展望： 展望明年 ， 我們認為 ： (1)AI需求維持強勁且應用持續擴大 ，
預期記憶體供不應求之情況將至少延續至 2027年，其中市場預估明年DRAM
報價將延續漲勢；(2)邏輯業務中，PMIC、分離式元件等8吋產能持續受惠AI強
勁需求，我們預期明年台系成熟製程廠商產能利用率有望達90%以上，公司規
劃明年漲價約10~20%且產能將以LTA客戶為優先；(3)考量目前無塵室空間幾
乎用罄，公司將開始評估新廠並著重3D AI Foundry產能擴產，但計畫透過設
備租賃、客戶給付預付款形式由客戶參與部分投資金額，以此控制公司資本支
出。我們初步預估明年營收達1,241.8億元，年增60.3%，稅後EPS 10.65元。 
 投資風險 
記憶體報價下滑、3D AI Foundry進展不如預期。 
主要財務數據及預估值 
(單位:百萬元) 2022 2023 2024 2025 2026 2027 
營業收入 76,087 44,023 44,726 46,730 77,447 124,181

| 營運概況  
3Q26獲利優於預期 ：力積電公告3Q26自結營收213.4億元 ， 季增23.4% 、 年增80.3% ， 毛利率47.6% ， 稅後EPS 1.38
元，獲利表現優於預期，因毛利率受惠於平均ASP季增24%、產能利用率由2Q26的87%提升至3Q26的97%。 
4Q26營收雙位數成⻑，看好毛利率重回過往50%以上高峰：展望4Q26，基於：(1)記憶體大廠優先投資HBM並逐
步退出利基型DRAM市場，加上IPC、網通和醫療等應用支撐，使得力積電DRAM代工需求維持穩健，至於與美光
合作之1P製程進展順利，預計2Q27開始試產，Flash業務中，SLC NAND則因記憶體大廠先進製程產能排擠，獲得
車用、網通等小容量應用降規需求挹注，另公司維持MLC NAND今年底tape out之進度；(2)邏輯業務中，儘管消
費性電子需求不佳，但AI伺服器需求強勁持續支撐PMIC、分離式元件需求，其中公司PMIC代工服務已從0.15微米
切入55奈米並開始出貨，且獲客戶三年綁約，反映需求強勁，另考量中介層與Wafer on Wafer於新竹廠區陸續復
線，將有利於4Q26 AI Foundry營收動能回溫。綜上所述，公司估本季營收有望雙位數季增，我們則預估有望季增
18.3%至252.4億元，毛利率53.6%，回到過往50%以上之高峰水準，稅後EPS 1.86元。  
正向看待明年展望：展望明年，我們認為：(1)AI需求維持強勁且應用持續擴大，預期記憶體大廠優先投資先進製程
之策略不變，故認為整體記憶體市場供不應求之情況將至少延續至2027年，其中市場預估明年DRAM報價將延續
漲勢(但漲幅收斂)，隱含公司代工價格具上漲動能；(2)邏輯業務中，半導體庫存維持健康，且PMIC、分離式元件等
8吋產能持續受惠AI強勁需求(其中分離式元件80%產能已簽訂LTA) ， 我們預期明年台系成熟製程廠商產能利用率有
望達90%以上，基於產能吃緊將有利於明年成熟製程廠商議價能力，公司規劃明年漲價約10~20%且產能將以LTA
客戶為優先；(3)3D AI Foundry業務仍為未來投資主軸，且產能規劃與進展大致如期，唯⼀有較新進展之業務則為
Wafer on Wafer規劃1H27量產 ， 另公司考量目前無塵室空間幾乎用罄 ， 公司將開始評估新廠並著重3D AI Foundry
產能擴產，但計畫透過設備租賃、客戶給付預付款形式由客戶參與30~60%的投資金額，以此控制公司資本支出。
我們初步預估明年營收達1,241.8億元，年增60.3%，稅後EPS 10.65元。

Document 201: 9月工业企业利润数据点评：采矿业为利润主要拉动，企业库存持续累积

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月工业企业利润数据点评：采矿业为利润主要拉动，企业库存持续累积.pdf
Report source: 万联证券
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

[Table_RightTitle] 
宏观研究|宏观跟踪报告

[Table_Title] 
采矿业为利润主要拉动， 企业库存持续累积

[Table_ReportType] 
——9 月工业企业利润数据点评

[Table_ReportDate] 2026 年 10 月 27 日

[T able_Summary] ⚫ 企业利润增速持续放缓，营收增速上行。9 月规上工业企业利
润累计同比下跌 0.2 个百分点至 15.5%，当月同比增速由上月
的4.2%上行至14.4%。从不同类型企业来看，国企、股份制企
业利润修复上行，私企、外企利润放缓，企业杠杆率整体小幅
抬升，主要是外企和私企拉动。 
⚫ 营收利润率同比增长，工业品价格延续上行，工业生产增速季
节性回温。PPI 同比由3.8%上行至5%，输入性通胀压力持续，
地缘政治扰动下，PPI 预期延续高位。工业增加值同比由5.2%
上行至5.5%，工业生产季节性上行， 新旧经济生产分化格局边
际收敛。营业收入利润率累计值由5.66%上行至5.68%， 同比上
涨 0.42 个百分点。工业企业营业收入累计同比持平于6.6%，
生产回暖和价格上行带来一定拉动。 
⚫ 企业成本小幅回落，应收账款扩张压力持续。每百元资产实现的
营业收入上行，企业资产使用效率持续改善。每百元营业收入
中的成本回落，费用端保持稳定。营收好转带动企业周转提速，
但库存水平仍高。应收账款同比持续走高，应收账款平均回收
期环比回落，同比上行，资金周转效率依然偏低。 
⚫ 采矿业利润高位攀升，下游高端制造业利润放缓。煤炭、石油和
天然气开采为采矿业主要拉动项，非金属矿采选业拖累作用较
大。地产对上游黑色金属、非金属矿行业拖累明显。化工产业
链受制于价格变动和需求放缓，增速回落。有色金属在价格高
位回落和高基数效应影响下，利润增速高位持续放缓。中游设
备制造表现分化，出口强劲带来支撑。下游制造业中，基数走
高和价格支撑减弱影响下，计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造
业增速高位回落，仍为主要拉动项。销售走弱和行业价格竞争
激烈挤压了汽车的利润空间。运输设备制造业利润回落，主要
受订单交付节奏和基数效应扰动。消费品制造业表现分化。 
⚫ 价格和结构因素带动行业补库， 下游需求和价格仍将制约利润
表现。 工业企业产成品库存持续累积， 但累库速度趋势性放缓。
价格抬升和部分行业补库驱动库存上行，电子、化学原料等行
业在价格、需求等因素下库存累积。内需修复偏慢给营收带来
约束，上、下游企业价格分化的格局仍将持续。四季度财政资
金逐步落地，有望推动制造业和基建投资增长。未来仍需关注
AI 产业链需求放缓和行业利润高基数等带来的影响。 地缘政治
扰动短期难以消退，上游能源产品行业价格预期维持高位。  
⚫ 风险因素：地缘政治风险发展超预期，政策推进和落地不及预
期，地产下滑幅度超预期。

### Page 3

[Table_Pagehead]  
证券研究报告|宏观报告

万联证券研究所 www.wlzq.cn 第 3 页 共 7 页  
事件 
2026年1-9月，全国规模以上工业企业实现利润总额6.03万亿元，累计同比较上月下
跌0.2个百分点至15.5%，9月当月同比增速由上月的4.2%上行至14.4%。

1    企业利润当月同比反弹，工业品价格保持增长 
企业利润当月同比反弹上行， 营收增速高位持平， 营收利润率同比保持上涨。2026年
9月， 全国规模以上工业企业利润累计同比由8月的15.7%继续回落至15.5%， 当月同比
增速为14.4%，前值为4.2%。规模以上企业营业收入累计同比持平于上月的6.6%，营
业收入利润率累计值由上月的5.66%上行至5.68%， 环比季节性上行， 同比上涨0.42个
百分点。 
工业品价格延续上行， 工业生产增速季节性回温。9月，PPI同比由3.8%上行至5%，累
计同比由2%继续上行至2.3%， 原油价格上涨带来的输入性通胀压力持续， 上游原料加
工企业利润相对占优， 下游企业利润承压， 行业利润分化格局持续。地缘政治局势依
然紧张，能化产业链价格预期维持高位震荡，PPI或将延续高位。工业增加值同比由
5.2%上行至5.5%，工业生产季节性上行，新旧经济生产分化格局边际收敛。

图表1：工业企业利润当月同比增速大幅反弹（%） 图表2：拆分看，工业品价格保持上行（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

企业营收持续修复， 成本小幅回落， 应收账款扩张压力持续， 费用端保持稳定。9月，
企业每百元资产实现的营业收入由72.4元上行至73.1元，企业资产使用效率持续改
善。每百元营业收入中的成本回落0.05元至85.02元，资源品价格高位给企业成本端
带来的压力持续。企业每百元营业收入中的费用持平于上期的8.35元。 产成品周转天
数由21.3天回落为21天， 企业周转边际提速， 但库存水平仍高。 规模以上工业企业应
收账款同比由8月的9%上行至10%，企业应收账款平均回收期环比由72.2天下行为71.6
天， 较去年同期的69.2天上行， 企业资金周转效率依然偏低， 并且企业应收账款规模
扩张的压力依然较大。

3    企业库存持续累积 
价格和结构因素带动行业补库， 下游需求和价格仍将制约利润表现。工业企业产成品
存货累计同比由11.0%继续上行至11.6%， 库存持续累积， 但累库速度趋势性放缓。价
格抬升和部分行业补库驱动库存增速上行， 电子、 化学原料等行业在价格和需求等因
素下库存累积。工业企业营业收入累计同比持平于6.6%，生产回暖和价格上行带来一
定拉动。内需修复偏慢给营收增速带来约束， 上、 下游企业价格分化的格局仍将持续。
四季度财政资金逐步落地， 有望推动制造业和基建投资增长。 未来仍需关注AI产业链
需求放缓和行业利润高基数等带来的影响。 地缘政治扰动短期难以消退， 上游能源产
品行业价格预期维持高位。

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for
every nuance. Lately, there seems to have been more RBA
communication to pore over. The method of delivery has
also evolved. This has sometimes caused confusion when
tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different

tone and varying complexity.
• The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly
using speeches directed to professional audiences to
discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such
as neutral rates and financial conditions. They are also
highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more
ways than just squeezing mortgage holders.
• One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises about the future

path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running
commentary about how it is interpreting recent data. These
interpretations are themselves useful signals about what
the RBA thinks it might do.
Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks
for clues to future rate moves. They will have plenty to pore over
in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry
of fireside chats this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded

interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday. Next
Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before
Senate Estimates, just as the quarterly CPI is released. (This
timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After
the decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a
media release and press conference. More speeches will follow,
including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just

following the RBA Review but also more recently. There may
be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication
outside that certainly feels more frequent than a couple of years
ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little sparse
outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary
appearances. We have counted 58 public engagements so
far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website.
This compares with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in

2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events
were related to economics, markets and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed
as well. There are fewer set-piece speeches on policy and a
Open mouth operations
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few
years ago, there are more interviews across a wide range of

media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It was
a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the
7:30 Report ; it is hard to imagine him agreeing to an Assistant
Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general
audience. Central banks want to explain why they do what
they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising
but the household sector is not the source of the inflation.

Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes
confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief Communication Officer
highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting
media conference is designed to speak to the public. Market
participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
the primary audience. So, if you think that what the Governor
said in the media conference sounded more ‘dovish’ than
what another official says in a more market-oriented forum,

it is partly because she is trying to convey that the RBA
understands that its recent policy actions are making things
harder for many Australians. But this is in much the same way
that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It does, but that
will not stop the treatment.
A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted
material such as the minutes and set-piece speeches than in
unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be

careful with their words in unscripted settings. If the tone
differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have
reduced the pressure to make the set-piece speeches more
accessible. Research on central bank communication often
uses measures such as the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and
Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was

beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison
suggests four speeches from 2026 ( 1, 2, 3, 4) were harder
to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches
scored in the 11 to 12 range, which is high-school-level
“The RBA does not want to promise what it will
do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks. "

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and
15, which is academic or technically dense text requiring
university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-
Governor Phil Lowe also scored better on these metrics than

the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that
readability metrics do not necessarily imply better clarity if the
concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has
evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has begun to put
more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those
invisible ‘star’ variables (NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial
conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences

has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on
the full range of transmission channels from interest rates to
inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour
supply channel). This is understandable in a world of supply
shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt
they would be comforted, though, by the Deputy Governor’s

explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the
smallest of the transmission channels from interest rates
to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on
inflation. It raises the question of whether other policy tools
or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on
inflation through the other channels without hitting a minority
of households disproportionately.

We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases
to summarise the position. From ‘narrow path’ to ‘not
ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once
entrenched, a phrase can outlast its usefulness.
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record
the presentations to the Board and the members’ discussion. The
minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-
meeting statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the

Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The August 2024 minutes
were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how
sensitive the forecasts were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed
Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s
communication have not changed. It wants to explain – and
perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how
it interprets incoming data, especially inflation, the labour

market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ about future moves.
Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular
future path for interest rates. Some academic literature argues
that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high,
for that matter) independently amplifies the transmission
of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-period

interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield
curve. Central banks including the RBA have certainly tried
jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view,
though, is that the potential cost of reputational loss if things
do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a
slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides
the signal about future policy directions. This is why it was

noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour
market as ‘a little tight’ after the September meeting, even
though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We
have been noting for some time that the RBA staff have a
downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the RBA’s
oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy
Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not surprise us.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
While annual retail sales growth improved in September,
rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace of growth
is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very
weak versus history. Consumer spending continues to be

constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to
employment prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five
years into the property downturn, house prices continue to
decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep
downturn, –20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from
annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in high-tech
investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst
domestically oriented businesses as well as property’s

malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and
2028 as the contribution from net exports moderates, broad-
based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect recent
targeted support measures to be broadened and given much
greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr
in September, exceeding both market expectations and the
Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely
driven by energy, fuel prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core

inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services
inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an
immediate concern inflation, the labour market showing
resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to
4.7% in August, suggests demand risks for inflation will need
close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as
the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is released. For both events,

the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The
week after, the US mid-term elections are due.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’
attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor Hauser's fireside
chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a
follow-up rate hike at the November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook
was "materially worse" than the RBA's August forecasts.

Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having
"crystallised" ahead of the September meeting – the Middle
East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser
noted the Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices
are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in August”.
He then positioned AI and data centre developments among
an "extraordinary sequence of positive demand shocks around
the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through

into CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy
Governor noted that "inflation's been coming in at or a little
bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the
labour market". He went on to state that the staff are currently
assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement
on capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other
words, revise up their assumption for the NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of

market pricing for a follow-up November hike, from just a
20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition
today ahead of next week's Q3 CPI. For that release, we are
forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a
0.9 (3.5%yr) increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview
provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3 as
well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast.
Provided inflation prints broadly in line with our expectations,

the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.
Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3
and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to 3.7%yr. Under
the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely
watched measures of core inflation took a step higher to now
be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our
NZ economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise
the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October policy review, while

retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.
Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised  to the upside in
Q3, GDP growth accelerating from 0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting
annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on
track to achieve their 4.5–5.0% full-year growth objective.
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
RBNZ now expected to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ will deliver a 25bp hike at its
policy review on 28 October. While September quarter
inflation was slightly below the RBNZ’s forecast, the
underlying detail was firm. More recently, increases in

fuel prices point to a material lift in inflation by year-end.
We expect further interest rate increases over the coming
months, with the OCR reaching a peak of 4% next year.
Softer headline inflation but firm core pressures
leave RBNZ on course to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ to raise the Official Cash Rate
by 25bp at its October policy review (previously, we had
expected a pause at this meeting). This change in view reflects
significant upside risks to near-term inflation relative to the

RBNZ’s previous forecast.
While in line with our forecast, the September quarter inflation
figures were slightly softer than the RBNZ expected, with prices
rising 3.7% over the past year (compared with the RBNZ’s
forecast of 3.9%). However, the details were less reassuring.
Excluding volatile items such as food and fuel, underlying
inflation pressures remained firm. Closely watched measures
of core inflation moved higher and now sit close to, or a little

above, the top of the RBNZ’s target band. We also saw an
increase in the number of goods recording price rises, taking
that measure back to levels seen in the wake of the pandemic.
Alongside the existing firmness in underlying inflation, the
risks for inflation over the coming quarters are firmly tilted to
the upside. That near-term upside risk has also been noted by
the Governor. Following a sharp rise in global fuel costs, we
expect inflation to reach 4.3% by the end of this year (above

the RBNZ’s September MPS forecast for +3.9%). While that is
mainly due to higher prices at the pump, businesses in sectors
such as construction are also reporting related increases in
transport and materials costs. Consistent with those trends,
we expect core inflation to remain in the upper part of the
RBNZ’s target band through 2027.
Reinforcing the case for an OCR hike at the October meeting,
the New Zealand dollar is notably weaker than the RBNZ

assumed: on a trade-weighted basis, the NZD is currently
3.6% below the RBNZ’s September projections. If sustained,
that would add to inflation pressures over the next few
quarters. We suspect this will not have shifted the RBNZ’s
medium-term outlook for inflation.
Several other developments need to be weighed against that
firmer inflation outlook. First, wholesale long-term interest rates
have risen markedly, and there has also been a more moderate

rise in some mortgage rates. The RBNZ is likely to note the role of
those increases in managing medium-term inflation risks.
In addition, the RBNZ is likely to continue to emphasise that
there is little evidence of a lift in businesses’ longer-term plans
for price setting at this stage in response to the rise in energy
prices (sometimes referred to as “second-round inflation
effects”). We expect the RBNZ to highlight the risk that more
persistent inflation pressures could emerge, depending on the

duration of the oil price shock.
We also expect the RBNZ to note that the labour market
remains cyclically weak and is not a significant driver of inflation.
Employment growth that remains weak relative to labour force
growth, subdued growth in job vacancies and low labour market
turnover are consistent with this view and could be emphasised.
Finally, the RBNZ is likely to acknowledge that while the
global growth outlook remains solid, there are significant

downside risks from the Iran war and concerns about the
sustainability of the AI investment cycle.
Balancing those concerns, we think the MPC will lift the
cash rate next week. However, a vote seems very possible as
differences in view will likely remain among the MPC about
how much the medium-term inflation outlook has really
shifted in recent weeks.
In September, the bulk of the MPC saw upside risks to the
inflation outlook while two members (Conway and Hansen)

thought the risks were balanced. Recent developments will
not have made those seeing upside risks to inflation more
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
comfortable, while the more dovish MPC members will
likely focus on the ongoing downside risks for growth given
continued softness in house prices and the mounting pressure
on real incomes. Indeed, all MPC members saw some degree
of downside risks to growth which likely remain relevant. All

these messages should remain in the RBNZ’s updated views.
The MPC is likely to continue to present its decision as a
gradual evolution of the approach taken since July: a higher
OCR now provides insurance against the need for a much
higher OCR later if upside risks to medium-term inflation
materialise. We doubt the aim will be to push market interest
rates materially higher.
RBNZ to leave door open for further hikes, OCR
still expected to peak at 4% in 2027

In its forward guidance, the RBNZ is likely to leave open the option
of raising the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy
Statement, without presenting an increase as certain. While the
RBNZ will not publish an updated OCR projection at this review,
the press statement is likely to note that further increases may
be needed, depending on economic conditions. The September
projection showed the OCR eventually rising to 3.25%.
We expect the RBNZ will end up delivering another 25bp

hike in December, followed by a further hike in February.
That would take the OCR to 3.5%, roughly where the RBNZ
estimates the neutral rate to be. At that point, we expect the
RBNZ to slow the pace of rate increases (previously, we had
expected that point to be reached a little later).
We continue to expect further 25bp hikes at the June and
September 2027 meetings, taking the OCR to a peak of 4.0%.
While oil prices are generating significant swings in inflation,

underlying pressures remain firm. More restrictive conditions are
likely to be needed to bring core inflation sustainably back to 2%.
Scenarios
Our central scenario, with a probability of around 60%, is that
the RBNZ increases the OCR by 25bp and signals that a further
increase in December remains an option, without committing to
a markedly tighter future stance than it outlined in September.
Alternative scenarios include:
• A hawkish scenario (around a 20% probability) would see

the OCR increase to 3%, with the RBNZ placing greater
emphasis on upside risks to core and medium-term inflation.
That would suggest an upward revision to the RBNZ’s
projected OCR path in December. Downside risks to the
exchange rate could also be emphasised.
• A dovish scenario (around a 20% probability) would
see the RBNZ leave the OCR unchanged and signal that
the medium-term inflation outlook has not changed
meaningfully from its September forecast, despite higher

near-term inflation. In this scenario, the RBNZ would
reiterate its previous forecast: a 25bp increase in December
and one further hike during 2027.
How might the election play into the decision?
While next week’s meeting is close to the General Election
on 7 November, the decision taken by the MPC will not be
politically motivated. However, the election outcome is close.
And should it result in a Labour Party-led coalition, it seems
likely the new government would adopt the Labour Party’s

promise to restore the RBNZ’s dual mandate. That would
require the MPC to weigh the degree of labour market slack
more highly in its deliberations. As the degree of labour market
slack currently seems high, a new mandate might justify the
MPC taking more time to return inflation to the middle of the
target range (our earlier note discusses this issue). This could
figure into the deliberations of some MPC members if the
decision between leaving the OCR unchanged and raising it

now is viewed as finely balanced.

AUS: Sep Private Sector Credit (%mth)
Oct 30, Last: 0.6, Westpac f/c: 0.5
Market f/c: 0.5, Range: 0.4 to 0.6
Private sector credit growth came in slightly firmer than
expected at 0.6%mth in August, above Westpac’s and the
market’s forecast for a 0.5%mth gain, leaving credit growth

running at 8.4%yr. Growth in housing credit continued to ease,
as we expected, though the mix highlights that the waning in
momentum is uneven, centred on investors rather than owner-
occupiers. Business credit meanwhile remains a source of
strength, notwithstanding some slowing recently.
For September, we expect credit growth to nudge to 0.5%mth,
with the divergence between housing credit and business
credit persisting.
AUS: Q3/Sep CPI (%qtr/%mth)

Oct 28, Quarter, Last: 0.6%, Westpac f/c: 1.3%,
Market f/c: 1.3%, Range: 0.6 to 1.4.
Oct 28, Month, Last: 0.4%, Westpac f/c: 0.6%,
Market f/c: 0.6 Range: 0.3 to 0.8
As noted in the review of the August CPI, our September
quarter CPI estimate is unchanged at 1.3%qtr holding the
annual pace flat at 3.9%yr. At the margin there was a 0.04ppt
increase due to a lift in auto fuel but it still rounds to 1.3%.
The seasonally adjusted CPI gains 1.1% highlighting that part

of the increase is due to usual seasonality.
In the month, the September CPI is expected to rise 0.6% with
the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr. Recreation & culture make
the largest contribution but transport is not far behind. Our
seasonally adjusted estimate increases 0.5%.
For more details please see “ September CPI Preview “.

Oct 28, Month, Last: 0.2%, Westpac f/c: 0.3%,

Our September quarter Trimmed Mean estimate is unchanged
at 0.9%qtr with the annual pace dipping a touch from 3.6%yr

which will be the fourth consecutive month at this pace. The
distribution of the September Trimmed Mean ranges from
–0.3% to 0.7%.

NZ: RBNZ Official Cash Rate Review (%)
Oct 28, Last: 2.75, Westpac f/c: 3.00, Mkt f/c: 3.00
We expect the RBNZ to raise the OCR by 25bps to 3% at
the 28 October Review. Data since September show a higher
outlook for year-end inflation and a stronger GDP starting

point, but increased risks to domestic consumption and
residential investment. On balance, the medium-term inflation
picture will probably be judged to have worsened slightly
given recent movements in energy prices and the exchange
rate. A vote seems very possible as differences in view will
likely remain among the MPC about how much the medium-
term inflation outlook has really shifted in recent weeks.
Forward guidance will likely leave open the option to raise

the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy Statement
without presenting it as a sure thing.
NZ: Oct ANZ Business Confidence (index)
Oct 29, Last: 51.9
Firms remained optimistic in the September survey of business
confidence, with both the activity and inflation measures
broadly holding steady. However, it’s likely that most responses
were submitted in the early part of the month, while fuel prices
took a sharp step higher from the second half of September. The

survey comes too late for the RBNZ’s October policy review, but
evidence about the breadth of cost and price pressures could
have a bearing on the RBNZ’s thinking about the total amount of
policy tightening needed in the months ahead.
NZ: Sep Monthly Employment Indicator (%mth)
Oct 28, Last: 0.2, Westpac f/c: 0.3
The MEI has been gradually ticking higher in recent months,
showing that the jobs market has held its ground through
the Iran conflict and the consequent surge in fuel prices. The

weekly snapshots provided by Stats NZ have been running
ahead of year-ago levels and suggest another solid result for
September – we’ve pencilled in a 0.3% increase. Given the
history of this measure, however, both this month and the
previous month are likely to be revised down a little from their
initial release.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Oct FOMC meeting
Oct 27-28, Last: 3.875%, Mkt f/c: 3.875%, WBC f/c: 3.875%
Core CPI inflation and producer price inflation both surprised
to the upside in September, and the price of Brent oil has
risen back to around USD110. Underlying momentum in
the economy also remains robust, although employment

disappointed in the latest update. Given the concerns shown
by Committee members over price risks and their collective
resolve to return annual inflation to target, the above data
could easily justify a follow-up hike at the October meeting.
That said, Committee members who have spoken recently
have shown patience with respect to assessing the economy
and positioing policy. The market has responded, effectively
pricing out the chance of an October hike in favour of a

December move, which is also our expectation.
US Q3 GDP
Oct 29, %ann’d, Last: 2.2%, Mkt f/c: 3.0%, WBC f/c: 3.1%
The US economy continues to grow at a pace well above trend.
This outperformance is almost entirely due to the strength of
investment in data centres and other AI related assets. The
pipeline for this activity remains strong, limiting near-term
risks to the downside.
Abstracting from AI investment’s contribution, other business
investment and residential investment is weak, weighed down

by high term interest rates and uncertainty related to trade
policy. Household consumption growth is volatile month-to-
month, but the trend is solid. Absent a marked deterioration
in the labour market, which is not our expectation, this
momentum is likely to persist, as wealth continues to grow and
household income gains neutralises inflation. Come 2027 and
2028, growth is expected to slow to trend, but not below.

### Page 1

CPO相關設備趨勢與台廠投資機會
福邦投顧研究部
2026/10

### Page 2

2【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
結論
◼ 需求強勁
CPO 自 2027 年進入放量期，光引擎需求 2026-2028 年由 62 萬顆增至 3,500 萬顆：AI 瓶頸由算力轉向傳輸，
Pluggable 於 3.2T 世代碰到功耗與密度上限，Scale-out 交換機率先轉向 CPO，2028 年起由 Rubin Ultra NVL576
跨櫃 Scale-up 接棒。OE 占 CPO 交換機 BOM 約四成，為價值鏈核心。
◼ 關鍵瓶頸
瓶頸測試與耦合為量產瓶頸，2028 年測試設備 TAM 約 418 億元、逾七成在 Ins3/Ins4：每顆 OE 須以次微米精
度主動對光，且模組良率隨 OE 數量倍數下滑，每一關都必須篩出良品，使 OE 成本近六成落在後段。Ins2 若
跳過，2027/2028 年整體 TAM 縮減 12%/16%，但 Ins3 測試機與貼合機需求反增。
◼ 台廠機會
台廠以致茂、鴻勁最確定，萬潤受惠貼合，其餘仍屬驗證期選擇權：致茂的 Ins3 OE 測試機已取得 2027 年量產

訂單，加上 Ins4 光學測試，預估 2027 年 CPO 營收 65-75 億元；鴻勁以光電同測 Handler 切入 Ins4，預估 55-60
億元；萬潤是 FAU/receptacle 耦合貼合的主要供應商，若採 receptacle 兩段貼合，貼合機需求最高可倍增。漢測
、旺矽、光焱仍在驗證階段，能否放量取決於 Ins2 的去留與台積電 HVM 驗證結果。
◼ 看好個股
致茂 (2360)、鴻勁 (7769)、萬潤 (6187)

### Page 5

5【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
CSP資本支出今明年延續高成長，支出重心可能逐步轉向網通設備
資料來源：BBG；福邦投顧整理
2026-27年CSP資本支出續強 ，主權AI投資有望接棒支撐 2028年成長：五大CSP資本支出於2026年年增約98%，
2027年仍有望成長47%，惟隨基期墊高，2028年年增率預估放緩至11%。但觀察到近期中東、歐洲等地主權AI計
畫與Neo cloud陸續宣布更多的投資及合作 ，中國亦積極布局AI資料中心，有望拓展CSP以外的需求來源，延續
整體AI基礎建設投資動能。
記憶體成本攀升 ，AI資本支出有望逐步向網通設備傾斜： 2025年資料中心成本中 ，IT設備約占整體支出 84%，
其中伺服器約占IT設備8成，網通與存儲設備則占其餘部分 。隨記憶體價格上漲、BOM成本攀升，NVIDIA已開
始調整Rubin Ultra的HBM規格，以優化整體成本。且若單顆GPU記憶體容量受限，MoE模型參數分配、Prefill /
Decode分離及KV Cache跨節點搬移等需求，可能推升GPU間的資料傳輸量，推升高速網路的重要性。

### Page 6

6【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
AI 算 力 成 長 超 越 資 料 傳 輸 能 力， 高 速 互 聯 成 為 系 統 效 能 關 鍵
資料來源：AI and Memory Wall；Nvidia；福邦投顧整理
AI晶片算力成長快於記憶體頻寬與互聯傳輸 ，系統瓶頸由「算不夠快」轉向「資料送不夠快」： 隨前沿模型參
數規模超越單顆GPU記憶體容量，須透過張量平行等方式拆分至多顆GPU協同運算，增加跨GPU資料交換需求。
根據學界統計，過去20年硬體峰值算力約每 2年成長3倍，DRAM頻寬與互聯頻寬卻僅成長 1.6倍及1.4倍，差距持
續擴大，使GPU等待資料的時間增加，形成「I/O Wall」。
推論需求由MoE延伸至超長上下文 ，任務碎片化與資料搬移推升互聯頻寬需求： MoE專家數量增至300個以上，
加上推論任務更加碎片化，需透過GPU間all-to-all通訊，進而推升Scale-up頻寬需求。進入長上下文階段後，影音
生成與軟體開發等應用的上下文長度突破100萬個token，帶動KV cache大幅增長；在GPU記憶體容量有限及Prefill

/ Decode分離部署下，資料須跨GPU與節點搬移，將推升Scale-out頻寬需求。隨模型複雜度與資料傳輸需求持續
提升，高速互聯成為避免GPU閒置、支撐AI效能成長的關鍵，也為網通設備投資提供長期動能。
圖3、I/O牆逐步成為算力瓶頸 圖4、推論複雜度由Dense演進至MoE與超長上下文

### Page 7

7【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
Scale-out Switch逐漸轉向CPO，NVIDIA、Broadcom產品2027年放量
資料來源：SemiAnalysis；Broadcom；福邦投顧整理
功耗與密度雙重瓶頸，推動Scale-out交換機由Pluggable轉向CPO： 在功耗方面，Pluggable鏈路需經PCB走線
與DSP進行訊號傳輸及補償 ，隨頻寬提升，Insertion Loss增加，訊號處理功耗也隨之攀升； CPO將OE直接整
合於Switch ASIC周圍，使訊號離開晶片後即可轉為光，減少訊號處理環節。在密度方面，GPU數量增加帶動
Port需求成長，但Pluggable受限於前面板插槽空間；CPO則將OE配置於ASIC周圍，突破前面板的密度限制。
目前CPO應用以NVIDIA為主、Broadcom其次，Spectrum-X推出有望帶動2027年擴大部署：CPO目前主要應
用於 Scale-out交換機 。NVIDIA轉向開放式乙太網架構後 ，客戶接受度提升 ，已知客戶包括 CoreWeave、

Lambda等Neo cloud及Oracle；隨Spectrum-X於2H26推出，預期2027年將擴大部署 。相較之下 ，Broadcom的
CPO交換機目前以Meta為主要客戶，多數雲端業者仍多採用純電版本，CPO尚未大規模導入。
圖5、CPO與DSP可插拔光模組訊號路徑差異 表1、Nvidia、Broadcom CPO交換機新產品

### Page 8

8【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
Rubin Ultra將導入光互聯Scale-up，Feynman可望全面轉向CPO
資料來源：SemiAnalysis；福邦投顧整理
Scale-up轉光最快有望於Rubin Ultra NVL576落地：MoE模型架構的all-to-all通訊推動Scale-up網域由72顆GPU
擴大至576顆，需串聯8個機櫃形成單一NVLink網域。目前機櫃內仍以銅背板互連為主 ，跨機櫃則導入光互聯
，NPO（Socket）與CPO方案均在開發中。Rubin Ultra採用9+9+9的Oberon機櫃設計，中間9個Switch Tray各配
置4顆NVLink Switch ASIC，每顆ASIC搭配4顆3.2T OE，推算每櫃配置144顆OE、每組NVL576共1,152顆。
Feynman NVL1152機櫃間將全面導入CPO：下一代Feynman NVL1152將由8個Kyber機櫃組成，機櫃間同樣採
用CPO；NVIDIA Roadmap亦已明確規劃Feynman將採用NVLink 8，並於機櫃間互聯全面導入CPO，機櫃內則

可能由銅互聯轉為CPO。

### Page 9

9【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
隨CPO / NPO Switch 逐步放量，OE需求有望於2026–2028年快速攀升
資料來源：Nvidia、台積電、廣發、福邦投顧整理預估
台廠Scale-out為2026–2028年CPO主要應用 ，2027年OE需求有望年增逾 18倍：以三層 Fat-Tree架構及 Spectrum
6800/6810為基礎推估，假設NVIDIA於2026/2027/2028年的CPO滲透率分別為10%/30%/50%，Scale-out CPO交換
機出貨量約為 4.8/91.8/154.7k台，對應OE需求約616/11,748/19,800k顆，2027/2028年年增率分別約 1,807%/69%。
預估2026–2027年CPO OE需求將主要來自Scale-out應用。
Scale-up初期以NPO導入，帶動2028年OE總需求上看3,500萬顆： 以Rubin Ultra NVL576每櫃配置144顆OE估算
，並考量良率及維修性需求，假設Scale-up初期採用NPO方案。若2027/2028年Rubin Ultra採用NVL576的比例分

別為 30%/50% ， 對應 Scale-up NPO OE 需求約 720/15,200k 顆 。 綜合 Scale-out CPO 與 Scale-up NPO 需求 ，
2026/2027/2028年OE總需求約為 616/12,468/35,000k顆，2027/2028年年增率分別約 1,924%/181%；其中CPO占比
將由100%降至94.2%及56.6%，顯示隨Scale-up自2028年起放量，NPO OE需求占比將明顯提升。

台積電主導 CPO 封裝，測試與耦合成量產瓶頸

### Page 11

11【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
Scale-out先行CPO，Scale-up因良率可能先採NPO過渡
圖8、從光模組到CPO的封裝工藝路線圖，過渡階段可能先以NPO的形式來做Scale-up
資料來源：TSMC、福邦投顧整理
Scale-out CPO現階段採OE on Substrate封裝：依台積電光互連技術路徑，目前Scale-out CPO交換機主要採OE
on Substrate架構，將OE與Switch ASIC共同封裝，尚未進展至將OE整合於Interposer的OOI階段。待封裝良率改
善後，OE有望進一步移至Interposer，相較現行CPO仍有約2.5倍的能效提升空間，為長期發展方向。
Scale-up初期可能先以Socket式NPO為主，以效能換取良率與維修性：NVL576跨櫃互聯提供CPO與NPO兩種方
案，CPO將OE直接封裝並外接雷射光源，NPO則透過Connector以Socket形式安裝OE，可單獨更換。CPO單一封
裝整合數十顆OE，任一顆失效即可能整顆報廢，良率隨OE數量增加而下降；NPO雖因多經Substrate走線與連接

器而效能略遜，但可避開此風險。

### Page 12

12【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
台積電COUPE拿下NVIDIA與Broadcom，CPO平台領先同業
圖9、各家矽光、封裝平台整理
資料來源：台積電、穎崴、福邦投顧整理
台積電以SoIC-X混合鍵合拿下NVIDIA與Broadcom兩大CPO客戶：SoIC-X將EIC直接鍵合於PIC上方，大幅降低
寄生效應，使NVIDIA得以實現200G/λ MRM，平台亦同時支援Broadcom的MZM路線。加上GC耦合支援晶圓級測
試與量產、完整PDK元件庫，以及由CPO延伸至OOI的路線圖，形成其他平台短期難以複製的整體方案 ，為目前
最佳的CPO量產平台。
新材料世代仍為其他平台留下切入機會：Broadcom陣營同步採用GF與Tower分散供應風險；若400G/λ世代須轉向
TFLN或InP等新材料，平台優勢可能重新洗牌。

### Page 13

13【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
台積電COUPE兼具GC與EC耦合，兼顧量產測試與低損耗
圖10、台積電COUPE-GC與COUPE-EC耦合架構
資料來源：台積電、福邦投顧整理
GC垂直耦合可做晶圓級測試 ，為COUPE率先量產的方案：COUPE-GC在PIC上製作光柵耦合器 ，光訊號垂直進
出，經Si Carrier上的Si lens聚焦並擴大對位公差，下方金屬反射層（MR）再回收散失的光以降低損耗。由於光從
晶圓表面進出，切割前即可逐顆量測，可直接銜接Insertion 1晶圓級測試。
EC邊緣耦合損耗更低，但須切割後才能對光：COUPE-EC由晶片側邊Facet進光，以SiN Tip轉換光模態進入波導
，損耗較低且對波長 、偏振較不敏感，利於往更高頻寬與 DWDM發展；惟須切割後才能耦光 ，Facet品質與對位
精度要求更高，測試須延後至Insertion 3。

### Page 14

14【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
光引擎佔CPO交換機價值40–50%，為最高價值零組件
圖11、CPO交換機價值鏈：OE 佔整機價值 40–50%
資料來源：GS、福邦投顧整理
OE為CPO交換機中價值最高的零組件 ，約占BOM成本4成：根據GS預估CPO交換機成本拆解來看，以NVIDIA
Quantum-X 交換機為例，總BOM成本約7.58萬美元，其中72顆光引擎合計約3.24萬美元，占BOM約43%，為4顆
Switch ASIC（占16%）的2.7倍；顯示CPO交換機的價值核心已由交換晶片轉向光學系統 。OE由PIC與EIC經
SoIC混合鍵合而成，負責電光轉換、調變與驅動，取代傳統可插拔光模組的核心功能；製程上須歷經 PIC與EIC
晶圓製造、混合鍵合、切割與receptacle耦光貼合，期間並穿插多道晶圓級與晶粒級光電測試 ，製程複雜度與附
加價值皆集中於OE。

### Page 15

15【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
OE成本近6成在封測，測試佔SiPh元件成本75%
圖12、OE成本結構、與SiPh元件製造成本拆解
資料來源：智璞、福邦投顧整理
OE成本近6成來自封裝與測試，晶圓製造僅約4成：以單顆OE約100美元拆解，PIC、EIC晶圓與SoIC堆疊
分別約占11%、17%、14%，合計約42%，其餘59%來自封裝與測試；若PIC或耦光良率偏低，報廢晶片將
推升晶圓端占比。
SiPh測試成本占比約75%，遠高於InP元件的29%：每顆die須先完成光纖對位才能量測，加上PDK未成熟
、多在切單後逐顆測試；若在晶圓階段完成KGD測試，占比可由封裝後的90%降至約60%，具晶圓級光測
與高精度對位能力的設備廠最受惠。

### Page 16

16【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
CPO封裝過程需經4道主要測試，以決定CPO量產良率
圖13、CPO光引擎4道測試流程與設備供應商
資料來源：富果、TrendForce、MoneyDJ、福邦投顧整理
CPO需額外加入光學測試，測試複雜度大幅提高：光電混合元件除電性外，還須驗證光訊號，凡涉及PIC的
測試都須先將光精準耦合進晶片，且OE封裝後難以重工，因此須在晶圓、Hybrid bonding、OE切割及模組組
裝完成等節點確保每顆OE皆為良品，由於光耦合對位耗時，量產瓶頸正由「對得準」轉向「測得快」。
CPO良率會隨OE數量呈倍數下滑： 一顆Switch ASIC周圍需搭載30顆以上的OE，假設單顆OE良率為95%，
以32顆計算，整顆CPO模組良率僅約19%；若單顆OE良率提升至99%，模組良率才能回升至約72%。任一顆
OE失效都可能導致整組模組報廢，因此必須在每道製程前先篩除不良品。
Insertion 1 Insertion 2 Insertion 3 Insertion 4

測試重點 分別對PIC、EIC兩種晶圓做wafer-level 測試 EIC Hybrid bonding在PIC上後，以上電下光做雙面wafer-level測試 切割後完整 OE (EIC + PIC + FAU) 測試；KGOE (Known Good OE) 確認光迴路 完整 CPO Module + Switch ASIC +多 OE系統測試整機鏈路驗證

17【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
Insertion 1：量產測試確定導入，測試時間有望大幅縮減
資料來源：Advantest、致茂、光焱、汎銓、TrendForce 、福邦投顧預估整理
PIC耦光對位為測試瓶頸 ，漏光預篩可望成為提速關鍵： Ins1於晶圓階段對EIC與PIC進行CP測試，EIC沿用一
般電性Probing，PIC則需在Prober加裝光學對位模組，量測光路損耗與MRM、PD等元件特性。由於每顆die都
須以次微米精度重新對準光纖，測試耗時成為量產瓶頸。業界正導入漏光定位設備作為預篩，先剔除漏光或有
潛在缺陷的die，免跑完整量測，以縮短整片晶圓的總測試時間 ；其中光焱NightJar 單格掃描不到1秒，量產版
NightJar-H正於台積電進行HVM驗證。
台廠方面：光焱可望受惠於預篩設備導入，致茂則以PIC整機方案送驗中，若通過驗證可望切入Insertion 1。
圖14、PIC檢測機台示意圖 圖15、PIC/EIC晶圓測試重點整理
項目
測試內容 EIC：同一般電性ProbingPIC：Prober加裝光學對位模組，打光量測損耗

核心目的 檢測光路損耗（耦合器、波導）及MRM、濾波元件、PD等元件特性
核心技術 6軸平台夾持光纖探針、可調雷射＋光功率計、波長掃描找微環共振；選配紅外線漏光定位
主要瓶頸 每顆die需重新對準光纖，次微米級閉環耦合，耗時過長
關鍵供應鏈 測試機：Teradyne、Advantest；探針台/卡：TEL、FormFactor；6軸平台：PI；光學量測：Keysight
市場現況 先以漏光定位篩出潛在缺陷die，PIC測試由100秒/die縮至<5秒/die

### Page 18

18【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
Insertion 2：技術尚未成熟仍待定，去留成觀察重點
資料來源：Advantest、Teradyne、旺矽、致茂、光焱、漢測、TrendForce ；福邦投顧整理
Ins2為CPO測試最難一站，雙面架構拖慢產出：Ins2於EIC以SoIC混合鍵合至PIC晶圓後、切割前進行測試，以上
電下光方式量測光迴路 、電光轉換之間的各種參數 。由於測試價值隨階段往後遞增 ，提前剔除不良die可提升後
續封裝良率，並劃分晶圓廠與OSAT之間的良率責任。此站需一面電性接觸、一面光耦合並同步精準對位 ，並兼
顧EIC發熱對Micro-Ring的影響 ，雙面架構難以多site平行測試，現行方案多為單site，單片晶圓測試約需6小時以
上，因耗時過長部分業者傾向暫時跳過，2027年放量看法分歧。
台廠方面：Advantest + 旺矽持續出二代機驗證中，漢測主要幫ficonTEC代工，光焱則提供快篩替代方案。
圖16、EIC/PIC D2W晶粒示意圖 圖17、EIC+PIC鍵合後晶圓測試重點整理

項目
測試內容 EIC以SoIC鍵合至PIC晶圓後、切割前，上電下光測E/O、O/E、O/O、光迴路及S參數
核心目的 測試價值隨階段遞增，提前剔除不良die以提升後續封裝良率
核心技術 雙面探測（一面電性接觸、一面光耦合）並同步對位；晶粒級溫控，避免EIC發熱影響微環共振
主要瓶頸 難以多site平行測試，多為單site、產出低；光耦合易受震動、溫度與翹曲影響

Insertion 3：Die Level全檢不可省，Shift to middle成CPO測試長期主力
資料來源：致茂、穎崴、萬潤、漢測、TrendForce；福邦投顧整理
Insertion 3為Die Level全檢，單顆取放拉長測試時間： Insertion 3於OE切割並貼合receptacle後進行測試，量測
光路品質（IL、PDL）與loopback誤碼率（BER），目的是避免切割、貼合過程產生的不良OE流入後續載板黏
合製程。相較Insertion 2的Wafer Level測試，Insertion 3須單顆取放、溫控與對接並全數檢測，未來可能每顆OE
的測試時間約為Insertion 2 per die的2–3倍。量產前全檢為必要條件，部分流程已導入大功率與老化測試，測試
時間仍有上升空間，但未來可能往多site平行測試發展以提升產出。
台廠方面：漢測（ficonTEC驗證中）、致茂(Tester出量產機)，萬潤則為FAU / receptacle耦合貼合主要供應商。

Insertion 4：Switch ASIC+OE成品測試，SLT為最後一塊拼圖
資料來源：台積電、鴻勁、致茂、TrendForce；福邦投顧整理
Insertion 4為完整CPO模組測試，SLT方案仍待成熟：Insertion 4於32顆OE貼合至載板、與ASIC共同封裝後進行
，驗證完整CPO模組的功能與連通性，涵蓋Ins4e電性最終測試、Ins4o光學連通性（插入損耗）、Ins4eo光電同測
（加測回送誤碼率 ），以及在接近實際運作狀態下跑真實流量的 SLT。預計未來將加入光電同測 、SLT，目前
Ins4eo連測試參數都尚未完全確定 。測試難點在於CPO元件尺寸大、功耗高，FAU設計不統一，散熱頭與光學對
接存在空間衝突，且ASIC與OE需雙區溫控；SLT更需供應大量外部雷射光源，測試複雜度仍高。
台廠方面：FT純電、Ins4eo以Advantest/Teradyne搭配鴻勁分選機為主，Ins4o致茂(tester)。

### Page 21

測試與貼合設備 TAM 擴張，台廠受惠Ins3、4

### Page 22

22【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
2028年測試設備TAM達400億元以上，Ins3、4占逾7成
資料來源：各公司、福邦投顧整理預估
TAM往後段集中 ，Ins3與Ins4為主要成長動能： 隨OE需求在2028年持續放大 ，測試設備 TAM明顯成長 ，其中
Insertion 3與Insertion 4的成長幅度皆高於整體，2028年合計占逾7成。Insertion 3須逐顆取放並全數檢測，是確保
Known Good OE的最後關卡；Insertion 4則須驗證整顆CPO封裝的光電功能，並逐步導入SLT，單顆測試價值最高
。若業界跳過Insertion 2，原本在晶圓階段的測試需求也將移往Insertion 3，進一步推升後段測試需求。
台廠以致茂、鴻勁為主要受惠者：致茂同時切入Insertion 3的OE測試與Insertion 4的光學測試及SLT；鴻勁以光電
同測Handler切入Insertion 4；穎崴、旺矽則分別提供測試座與晶粒探針台。

### Page 23

23【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
Ins2 不做可以節省成本，且隨 SoIC 製程良率提升測試時間減少
圖24、每產出一顆良品OE所需測試時間比較
資料來源：福邦投顧整理預估
SoIC 良率越高，不做 Ins2 每顆良品 OE 所需測試時間越少：不做 Ins 2 可省下每顆 die 在 Ins 2 的 45 秒，代價是
SoIC 不良品會額外占用 Ins 3 的測試時間，因此節省幅度取決於 SoIC 良率。良率約 60% 時兩者打平；良率越高
、不良品越少，每產出一顆良品 OE 所需的測試時間由良率 60% 時的 187.5 秒降至 80%/90% 時的 140.6/125.0 秒
，相較有做 Ins 2 分別節省約 28/38 秒（-17%/-23%）。
整體測試設備TAM縮減，但 Ins3 機台需求增加：若省略 Ins2，SoIC 後的良品與不良品 OE 都將直接進入 Ins3 受
測，受測量放大為 1÷SoIC 良率倍，Ins3 因受測 OE 增加，所需機台數也將大幅提升。但因省下整段 Ins2 機台，

整體而言 2027/2028 年測試設備新增 TAM 仍將分別減少約 12%/16%，可為客戶省下更多設備投資成本。
SoIC良率 有做Ins2 不做Ins2 每顆die節省時間
50% 202.5秒 225.0秒 多22.5秒（+11%）
60% 187.5秒 187.5秒 打平
70% 176.8秒 160.7秒 16.1秒（-9%）
80% 168.8秒 140.6秒 28.1秒（-17%）
90% 162.5秒 125.0秒 37.5秒（-23%）
100% 157.5秒 112.5秒 45.0秒（-29%）

致茂、鴻勁2027年CPO測試產值逾50億元，居台廠之首
資料來源：各家公司資料；福邦投顧整理預估
致茂、鴻勁卡位Insertion 3／4，2027年CPO測試營收領先：致茂布局橫跨Ins 1、3、4，其中Ins3的OE光電測試
機已通過驗證並取得 2027年量產訂單，Ins4o的光學連通性測試機亦已通過驗證 ，兩者合計預估2027年營收65-
75億元；鴻勁以CPO純電測Handler與光電同測Handler切入Ins 4，光電同測量產型驗證機已於2026年10月出貨。
漢測、旺矽、光焱多仍在驗證階段：漢測為ficonTEC在地量產夥伴，Ins2暫時仍面臨不採用，Ins3於ficonTEC驗
證中 ；旺矽與Advantest合作的雙面晶圓級探針台仍在量產驗證，晶粒對晶粒探針台則已出貨；光焱NightJar-H
正於台積電進行HVM驗證，若Triton放量，光焱模組可望隨之出貨。

### Page 25

25【僅供內部教育訓練使用，嚴禁外流 】
省略Ins2推升貼合機需求，2027年貼合機TAM增加約24%
圖26、FAU貼合位置示意圖
資料來源：TSMC、福邦投顧整理預估
FAU為光纖與PIC之間的耦合介面 ，現行有兩種貼合設計： FAU貼合於光引擎側邊 ，負責將光纖與PIC耦合
。現行設計分為兩種：先貼 receptacle再安裝FAU，以及直接將FAU貼合於OE上；前者可能需要兩道貼合製
程，後者僅需一次。
省略Ins2後不良品也須貼合 ，2027年貼合機需求增加約24%：以直接貼合FAU估算，有做Insertion 2時，僅
SoIC後的良品OE需要貼合；若省略Insertion 2，未經篩選的不良品也會一併進入貼合製程，使2027年貼合機
TAM增加約24%。隨SoIC良率提升、不良品減少，兩者差距逐步收斂 ，2028年已大致相當 。這也代表在量
產初期良率仍低時，省略Insertion 2的成本不只是測試，也會反映在貼合機台與貼合材料的額外消耗上。

Receptacle設計若需兩段貼合，2028年貼合機TAM有望達100億以上
資料來源： 萬潤、高明鐵、惠特；福邦投顧整理預估
Receptacle 設計有望使貼合機 TAM 最高倍增：以每次貼合約 3 分鐘估算，直接貼合 FAU 於 2027/2028 年新
增貼合機 TAM 約 43/51 億元；receptacle 設計須經 receptacle 貼合與 FAU 安裝兩段製程，若兩段皆需同等時間
的主動對位，TAM 最高可增至 85/101 億元。
台廠萬潤為主要受惠者，高明鐵、惠特送樣中：萬潤為目前最主要的 FAU 耦合貼合設備供應商，已出機至矽
品；若最終採 receptacle 兩段貼合設計，貼合機需求最高可倍增，萬潤作為現行主要供應商受惠幅度最大。其
他台廠方面，高明鐵、惠特目前均在送樣階段，國際競爭者則包括 MRSI 及港建代理的 ADST。

Doanh thu 2Q2026 giảm nhẹ do 
không còn ghi nhận doanh thu 1 lần 
từ phí nước thải; trong khi LNST 
công ty mẹ tăng trưởng 1 chữ số

Tiêu thụ nước khu vực dân sinh được 
thúc đẩy nhờ thời tiết nắng nóng 
hơn; trong khi triển vọng nhóm 
khách hàng công nghiệp bị ảnh 
hưởng bởi bất lợi thuế quan

Đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyện và xử 
lý rác thải được phê duyệt cho phép 
cải thiện doanh thu và lợi nhuận của

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
59,000 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu 2Q2026 đạt 1,222 tỷ (-8% YoY/18.1% QoQ) và LNST công ty mẹ đạt 325 tỷ 
VND (7% YoY/55% QoQ), ghi nhận tăng trưởng của tất cả các mảng. Mảng cung cấp 
nước tăng trưởng ổn định, tiếp tục là trụ cột chính của BWE, đóng góp doanh thu 
785 tỷ VND (+12% YoY/12% QoQ) nhờ nhà máy nước Long An vận hành từ Q3/2025 
giúp sản lượng nước tăng 28% YoY.

Cường độ và thời gian kéo dài của các đợt nắng nóng trong 2026/2027 là cơ sở để 
chúng tôi điều chỉnh tăng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước của nhóm khách hàng dân cư 
tại Bình Dương và Bình Phước trong năm 2026 - 2027 lên mức 7.5/6.5% YoY. Trái lại, 
triển vọng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước nhóm sản xuất ảm đạm do tình hình thuế 
quan có diễn biến bất lợi trong ngắn hạn cho các ngành xuất khẩu truyền thống 
của Việt Nam.

UBND TP.HCM công bố đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyển và xử lý rác thải mới, tăng 
mạnh từ 50-150% nhằm phản ánh đầy đủ và chính xác hơn các chi phí phát sinh 
trong thực tế. Chúng tôi cho rằng điều này sẽ tác động tích cực với BWE khi các dự 
án của doanh nghiệp ở mảng này sẽ vận hành hiệu quả hơn. Dự báo doanh thu và 
lợi nhuận gộp của mảng xử lý rác thải lần lượt đạt 1,069/1,234 và 165.7/180.7 tỷ 
VND lần lượt trong 2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% và 27%/9% YoY.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp định giá FCFF và EV/EBITDA, KBSV khuyến nghị MUA với 
BWE, giá mục tiêu 59,000 VND, tương đương với mức lợi suất kỳ vọng 42.2% so với 
giá đóng cửa ngày 22/09/2026.

Cơ cấu doanh thu 2025 Hoạt động kinh doanh 
 Biwase là doanh nghiệp hàng đầu 
trong lĩnh vực cung cấp nước sạch với 
thị phần đạt 100% tại Bình Dương. 
Ngoài ra, công ty còn mở rộng sang 
lĩnh vực đầu tư nhà máy xử lý nước và 
chất thải rắn nhằm hoàn thiện hệ 
sinh thái dịch vụ hạ tầng môi trường 
tại khu vực. 
Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Nắng nóng thúc đẩy tiêu thụ nước sạch khối dân cư, đem về tổng doanh thu cấp nước tại Bình Dương và 
Bình Phước năm 2026/2027 đạt 2,953/3,192 tỷ VND (6.8%/7.4%). 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Nhà máy nước Nhị Thành giai đoạn 3 đi vào hoạt động giúp tổng doanh thu nhà máy đạt lần lượt 213/247 tỷ 
VND (+16%/16% YoY) trong 2026/2027 và duy trì tăng trưởng trung bình 15% đến 2030.

tại đây

HOẠT ĐỘNG KINH DOANH 
Doanh thu 2Q2026 giảm nhẹ do 
không còn ghi nhận doanh thu 1 lần 
từ phí nước thải; trong khi LNST 
công ty mẹ tăng trưởng 1 chữ số 
 Doanh thu 2Q2026 đạt 1,222 tỷ (-8% YoY/18.1% QoQ) và LNST công ty mẹ đạt 
325 tỷ VND (7% YoY/55% QoQ). Mảng cung cấp nước tăng trưởng ổn định, tiếp 
tục là trụ cột chính của BWE, đóng góp doanh thu 785 tỷ VND (+12% YoY/12% 
QoQ) nhờ nhà máy nước Long An vận hành từ Q3/2025. Kinh doanh tăng trưởng 
tích cực là nhờ giá nước đầu vào mua từ TDM thấp hơn giúp tăng biên lợi nhuận 
gộp, cùng với không phát sinh lỗ tỷ giá như cùng kỳ năm ngoái.
